2. B.    10.04.11 
2. RECOGNITION 
B.   Advanced Placement Honors - Troy School District 
 More than 350 Troy students have been honored for their outstanding 
performance on Advanced Placemen t exams during the past school 
year.  Troy students were honored by the College Board in four award 
categories: AP Scholar, AP Scholar with Honors, AP Scholar with 
Distinction and National AP Scholar.   Tonight, we will be honoring 
students who have earned the Nation al AP Scholar honors. This award 
is granted to students who receive an average gr ade of four on all of 
the AP exams that were taken last year. Students in this category must 
also take eight or more AP exams during the school year.   
This evening, we invited students who achieved this honor during their 
junior year and those students are:  Sandeep Arakali, David Bai, Neha 
Bokil, Allen Chen, Rolland He, Darw in Hsu, Yujing Liang, and Clark 
Zhang.  
 Would these students please come forward at this time?  
Recommended Action:  Distribute Certificates

2. C.   10.04.11 
2. R ECOGNITION  
C.  2011-2012 National Merit Scholarship Semifinalists  
This year, more than one million stud ents nationwide from some 22,000 high 
schools across the nation entered the National Merit Scholarship Program; 
however, only 16,000 are selected to be semifinalists.  This evening we will be 
passing a resolution to commend and cong ratulate our fifty-two National Merit 
Scholarship Semifinalists. This is a rema rkable number of semifinalists and a 
tribute to the staff and pare nts of the Troy School Dist rict.   Copies of this 
resolution will be presented to the students at a recep tion for the students on 
November 9.

 2. D.   10.04.11  
2. RECOGNITION 
D.  RECOGNITION:    Nicholas Green Distinguished Student Award 
The National Association for Gifted Ch ildren (NAGC) sponsors the NAGC – 
Nicholas Green Distinguished Stud ent Awards.  The Nicholas Green 
Foundation was established by Maggie and Reg Green to honor the memory 
of their seven-year-old son, Nicholas, who was killed in a drive-by shooting 
while visiting Italy in 1994.  The Greens paired wi th NAGC to fund this 
awards program, which is designed to  recognize distingu ished achievement 
in academics, leadership, or the arts, in children grades 3 through 6.  Each 
year, one child in each state may be named a Nicholas Green Distinguished 
Student.  Each winner receives a $500 U.S. savings bo nd and an NAGC 
Certificate of Excellence.  This year, a sixth grade Boulan Park Middle School 
student received this award.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Introduce Elizabeth Br ooks, chair of the 
scholarship committee of Michigan Association for Gifted Children (MAGC), the 
state affiliate of the NAGC.   
Elizabeth will present the award to Brittany Ngia.  
10
Award Information: 
Nicholas Green Distinguished Student Awards 
The National Association for Gifted Children (NAGC) sponsors the NAGC - Nicholas Green 
Distinguished Student Awards. Originally funded by the Nicholas Green Foundation and 
NAGC, this awards program is designed to recognize distinguished achievement in academics, 
leadership, or the arts, in children grades 3 through 6. NAGC believes that the Nicholas Green 
Distinguished Student Awards inspire children to achieve to their fullest potential, highlight 
high-ability students, and draw attention to the educational needs of our nation's gifted and 
talented students. 
of their seven-year-old son Nicholas who was killed in a drive-by shooting while visiting Italy in 
1994. The Greens paired with NAGC to start the Distinguished Student Awards because they 
wanted to recognize young people who are: 1.) working hard to make the most of their lives to 
develop their unique gifts and talents and 2.) close to the age of Nicholas at the time of his death. 
Each year, one child in each state may be named a Nicholas Green Distinguished Student. Each 
winner receives a $500 U.S. savings bond and an NAGC Certificate of Excellence. Eligible 
students may be nominated by parents, teachers, students, or community/civic groups.  (Source: 
NAGC website) 
Award recipient: Brittany Ngia  
Brittany is the daughter of Lester Ngia and Natalie Lee. She is a sixth grade student at Boulan 
Park Middle School. Brittany was nominated for this award by her classroom teacher, Mrs. 
Suzanne Miller, of Hamilton Elementary School. She is an excellent student, both in academics 
and citizenship. She has often tutored classmates and served as a peer mediator and student 
council representative. Brittany is in advanced math classes and has received high honors on a 
number of state and competitive examinations. In the past, she has been recognized for her 
artistic skills, her writing ability, and her musical talent. Brittany plays two instruments and sits 
first chair in the Civic Youth Ensemble of the Detroit Symphony Orchestra. Her latest interests 
include Lego Robotics and assisting with disaster relief fundraising. 
former board member of MAGE (Michigan Alliance for Gifted Education), which is now known 
as MAGC (Michigan Association for Gifted Children), the state affiliate of the NAGC. She 
currently serves as chair of the scholarship committee of MAGC and is a graduate student at U 
of M Flint.

Ms.  Philippart  mentioned  the  Jumpstart  and  Adventures  in

support  programs  or  for  enrichment.   Mr.  Stromayer  indicated 
that Jumpstart   is used as a refresher to strengthen skills for the 
new  school  year  and  that  Adventures  in  Learning  is  more  of  an 
enrichment‐type program modeled after the PACE program.   
Ms. Philippart inquired as to the number of students enrolled in 
this  summer  program  who  participate  for  enrichment  purposes
compared  to  remediation.   Mr.  Stromayer  did  not  have  these 
statistics  but  indicated  that  many  students  are  academically  at 
risk  while  a  great  deal  attend  strictly  for  enrichment  and  to 
prepare  for  the  upcoming  school  year.   He  also  added  that  the 
district  offers  remedial  summer  programs  such  as  ESL  for  high 
school students, math labs and reading/writing classes for middle 
school and Jumpstart for elementary students.   
Ms. Philippart asked if we have the flexibility to deliver programs 
through the internet when we move forward.  Mr. Stromayer said 
the district is a little reluctant to do 100% internet due to the lack 
would be looking at programs such as NovaNET Courseware.  Ms.

addressed  the  district’s  AYP  status  and  the  fact  that  Larson  and

BUSINESS 
Consolidation of Services 
11‐089 
WHEREAS, MCL 388.1622f(1)(c) requires that if a district 
did  not  enter  into  an  agreement  with  the  Michigan  Department 
of  Education  (MDE)  to  develop  a  service  consolidation  plan  to 
reduce school operating costs under former section 11d as it was 
in effect for 2010‐2011, the district enters into an agreement with 
MDE to develop a service consolidation plan that is incompliance 
with department guidelines described in subsection (2); and;  
WHEREAS,  the  district  has  already  implemented  cost 
savings through cooperative purchasing and service consolidation 
initiatives; and;  
WHEREAS,  the  district  continues  to  pursue  any  and  all 
consolidation of services that make sense for the district without 
such agreement; and;  
WHEREAS,  the  district  intends  to  continue  to  realize 
savings  from  cooperative  agreements  and  service  consolidation 
plans currently in place; and;  
WHEREAS,  the  intent  is  for  Troy  School  District  to  fully 
qualify  for  the$100  per  pupil  incentive  payment  under  MCL 
388.1622f Best Practices Incentive Grants;  
NOW,  THEREFORE,  BE  IT  RESOLVED  THAT  the  Troy 
School  District  will  enter  into  an  agreement  with  the  Michigan 
Department of Education to develop a Service Consolidation Plan 
to reduce school operating costs that is in compliance with MDE 
Service Consolidation Guidance distributed on June30, 2011.  
Ayes:  7 
Nays:  0 
ATTACHMENT A 
SERVICE CONSOLIDATION PLAN AGREEMENT 
BETWEEN 
MICHIGAN DEPARTMENT OF EDUCATION  
AND  
TROY SCHOOL DISTRICT 
Troy  School  District  agrees  to  develop  a  Service  Consolidation 
Plan to reduce school operating costs that is compliant with MDE 
Service Consolidation Guidance distributed on June 30, 2011.  
The Troy School District Board of Education resolution approving 
this agreement is attached.

 7.C.   10.04.11 
7. CURRICULUM 
C. REPORT:  Troy Educator Appraisal Model (TEAM) 
During the second semester of the 2010-2011 school year, a committee 
comprised of eight members of the TEA and eight administrators was 
formed and charged with creating a ne w evaluation instrument that would 
comply with Michigan's education reform legislation.  TEAM (Troy Educator 
Appraisal Model) is the result of months of hard work and is representative 
of what can be accomplished through strong collaboration.  
This evening, the video that the committee produced to introduce 
TEAM to all TEA members at each  of the buildings’ opening staff 
meetings, will be shown.  As you will see, this video explains the 
process used to create TEAM an d shows the rubric that will be 
used in evaluation.   
 RECOMMENDED ACTION:  This evening’s report is presented by 
 Dr. Rich Machesky and Mrs. Karen Bush. 
130
Troy Educator Appraisal Model
Developed Jointly by TEA and TSD 
August 2011 
Monday, August 29, 2011 
131
TEAM 
Troy Educator Appraisal Model 
District Mission: 
The purpose of the Troy School District is to ensure learning for all members of the school 
community. 
Vision: 
The Troy School District will become an exemplary learning community that supports innovation 
and is committed to continuous improvement. The Troy School District will be a place where a 
collaborative community develops curriculum, instructional strategies, and assessment to ensure 
all students learn. 
Goals: 
All students will be on track to be college/career ready in literacy. 
All students will be on track to be college/career ready in mathematics. 
All students will be on track to be college/career ready in science. 
All students will be on track to be college/career ready in social studies. 
Shared Beliefs: 
 The Troy School District is committed to excellence. We celebrate our diverse 
community of learners and the unique qualities of each individual.  
 The educational process is a partnership with the entire community including family, 
business and community organizations. 
 The educational process will provide each student the best opportunity to learn, to 
develop personally, and to experience academic and personal success.  
 The achievement of all students is best accomplished by focused attention on best 
instructional practice enhanced through collaborative conversation on student work.  
Monday, August 29, 2011 
132
 Data is used as a tool for improvement, informs our decision making, indicates areas of 
success that are celebrated, and provides specific areas of weakness that must be 
strengthened. 
 Students must be prepared for successful post-secondary learning experiences. 
 Learning happens in and outside the classroom as evidenced through exceptional fine arts 
and athletic endeavors. 
 Accurate information is provided to all Troy Schools’ constituents that reflect goals, 
current achievement status, and strategic planning. 
Purpose of TEAM: 
The purpose of the Troy School District (TSD) Troy Educator Appraisal Model (TEAM) is to 
maintain a climate that ensures quality instruction and enhances the professional growth of all 
members of our professional community. The goal of this process is to ensure continuous 
improvement through the use of: 
 Research based instructional practice 
 Staff development aligned to common core and other nationally recognized curricular 
expectations 
 Administrative instructional leaders that promote and support instructional best practice 
The ultimate outcome is to develop the most highly regarded and high performing professional 
teaching staff. To this end, members of the professional teaching staff, along with central and 
building administration teams gathered during the spring of 2011 to collaboratively develop the 
following document.  
We believe in order to ensure learning for all members of the school community it is necessary 
we support the highest level of instructional practice throughout the TSD. Teacher preparation 
and performance has a significant impact on student achievement. Administrative support and 
understanding of instructional practice makes a significant contribution to teacher development 
that enhances student performance. This appraisal process recognizes that teachers are at 
various levels in their growth, therefore, flexibility and choice along with collegial and 
administrative support will best help our teachers meet their goals of succeeding proficiency.  
Monday, August 29, 2011 
133
Guiding Principles of the TEAM: 
 Growth and development are best achieved in an atmosphere of mutual trust and respect.  
 Central and building level leaders have an important responsibility to understand, promote 
and support best instructional practice at all levels.  
 Reflection and analysis are essential for the professional growth of teachers and their 
successful practice of teaching.  
 Professional practice that includes teachers learning together from one-another 
promotes shared expertise and ensures professional growth from within the organization.  
 Performance evaluation requires open communication and specific information that 
identifies behaviors that can be observed, recorded, analyzed, shared with others, 
replicated and adjusted when necessary. 
 TEAM is a road-map that provides options for professional growth, including prescriptive 
support for non-tenured staff, and those requiring more administrative and/or collegial 
support in sustaining their professional growth while other teachers are encouraged to 
expand their professional practice by choosing their individual evaluation plan.  
 The practice of teaching is both an art and science that evolves over many years of 
practice. Teachers, like students, never stop learning. We realize these truths by 
identifying three tracks to provide flexibility and support where necessary to assure the 
success of all teachers.  
 Probationary teachers benefit from specific observational data and analysis while 
tenured teachers may benefit from the support and encouragement as they reflect, 
research, and implement new methodologies with administrators as colleagues. Some 
teachers may require more support and or intensive intervention and assistance. This is 
made possible by instructional leaders whose responsibility it is to identify areas of 
weakness, mutually develop improvement plans and provide feedback and support toward 
implementation.  
 TEAM is subject to annual evaluation, validation, refinement and updating. 
Monday, August 29, 2011 
134
Component Parts: 
Levels of Performance:  
*According to the most recent legislation, district ratings must be cross-walked to the State of 
Michigan identified ratings and reported in the following categories at the end of the year.  
Troy Educator Appraisal Model (TEAM) – Proposed Plan 
standards during the period of performance.  
Gaining Proficiency– Teacher has demonstrated growth but the level of achievement has not 
yet achieved TSD standards during the period of performance. 
Proficient – Teacher demonstrated basic competence on identified TSD standards during the 
period of performance.  
Exceeds Proficiency - Teacher consistently and significantly exceeded competence on 
standards of TSD performance during the period of performance.

137
Memorandum 
 TO: Dr. Fowler 
  Superintendent 
Technology 
 FROM: Merri Lynn Colligan 
  Director of Technology  
Resource 
 Center 
 RE: Network Core Purchase 
Troy, MI 48098 
248/823-5090 
Background:
As the district moves toward a centralized server infrastructure and the Troy Cloud 
environment, the network infrastructure must be upgraded to meet the demands of the 
increased network bandwidth from client connections and infrastructure services. To 
accommodate the new requirements, the district needs to upgrade the connection 
speed in the core router from a 1 Gig to a 10 Gig connection.  
The existing 2 core routers run as separate entities and have no redundancy in the 
design. If one of the core 6509 routers were to fail, the other would not be able to take 
over the routing of traffic. The new design will allow the two core routers to backup the 
routing for each other.  
This upgrade will require the purchase of two cards for the 6509 core routers and an 
additional switch to interface with the district servers.  
The technology department reviewed pricing from many organizational cooperative bids 
including REMC, MICTA, and AEPA as well as other Oakland County cooperatively bid 
RFPs to ensure the District was receiving prices in accordance with the Board 
Purchasing Policy. 
Recommendation:
The Technology Department recommends the purchase of a 2 Cisco Nexus 5010 and 
two 6509 4 10 Supervisor 720 cards with 2 10 Gig ports.  
Please see attached detail regarding component specifications. 
Costs:
Company Cooperative Bid  Total Cost 
Netech MICTA $ 105,388.50 
Funding: This is a Bond Fund expenditure.

 DATE:  September 28, 2011

  The Technology Department is  recommending that the District 
replace the Network Area Storage as the current device has 
surpassed its useful life and the co st to maintain this equipment 
continues to increase.  Existing  bids through REMC, MICTA, and 
AEPA, as well as other Oakland County cooperatively bid RFP’s 
were reviewed by Mrs. Merri Lynn Colligan, Director of 
Technology, and it is recommended  that the bid be awarded as 
stated below.

140
Memorandum 
 TO: Dr. Fowler 
  Superintendent 
Technology 
 FROM: Merri Lynn Colligan 
Resource 
  Director of Technology  
 Center 
 RE: Storage and Backup Purchase 
4420 Livernois 
Troy, MI 48098 
 DATE:  September 28, 2011  
248/823-5090 
Background:
The district purchased a Network Area Storage in 2004. The useful life of a storage 
server is 5 years. This device has surpassed its useful life and the cost to maintain this 
older equipment continues to increase. In addition to the production storage, the district 
needs to increase its backup capacity to maintain data integrity on District systems. 
As the District implements the Troy Cloud (virtual desktops) and the need for more disk 
capacity increases across the District systems, it was determined a replacement system 
would be necessary.  
The Technology Department requested information from vendors on possible storage 
solutions, and reviewed and analyzed specifications of various Storage Area Network 
replacements. At the same time, the Technology Department dialogued with the 
Oakland ISD Technology Department to determine if this was a service which could be 
provided for a fee, but the cost was prohibitive and at this time the ISD does not feel it 
has available the requested disk space.  
After initial demo and pilot testing, the Dell Compellent and EMC solutions were 
selected for further review. The Technology Department requested references for the 
solutions to ensure that the technology selected had proven implementations in the K12 
environment as well as the infrastructure to expand disk capacity and adjust the disk 
type as needed.  
The EMC Solution was determined to be the preferred solution based on the following 
criteria: 
 Scalable to add disk capacity and support up to 4000 virtual desktops in the Troy 
Cloud. 
 Optimized for virtualization 
 Extendable cache 
 Optimized for flash  
 Proven integration with UCS platform and Cisco fiber channel connectivity 
 Guaranteed Up time  
 Local manufacturer support 
141
The Technology Department reviewed pricing from many organizational cooperative 
bids including REMC, MICTA, and AEPA as well as other Oakland County cooperatively 
bid RFP’s to ensure the District was receiving prices in accordance with the Board 
Purchasing Policy. 
Recommendation:
The Technology Department recommends the purchase of an EMC VMAXe, and an 
EMC Avamar Backup Server. 
Please see attached detail regarding component specifications. 
Costs:
Company Cooperative Bid  Total Cost 
CDWg  AEPA $ 636,000.00 
Funding: This is a Bond Fund expenditure.
